=== Qtempl hit 1 at 226707 ===
 The factor inventory is exhaustive: Wilson words, product-Haar coordinate factors, coarea
and normal returns, gauge-coordinate endpoints, Gaussian/Schur endpoints, inherited activities, cutoffs,
and source leaves are exactly the ownership inventory in row (U1) of Theorem 5.27, implemented by
the one-step maps (E1)–(E6), together with the labelled inner/collar/outer partition. The labels sum
pointwise to one before logarithms, so an atlas-boundary factor cannot be lost between branches.
   Definition 3.22 assigns every reported nonempty factor to precisely one of the regular, one-core, or
cross rows; Lemma 3.25 does the same for every connected determinant word. Exact collect then replaces
each history by one canonical hull. The history-compatibility theorem computes the next allocation only
from this collected output, so neither a factor nor its scalar normalization is copied at a later depth. This
gives (HT1) at j + 1. The normalized structural/source marks commute with the finite induction and are
bounded by the same permanent rows, proving depth uniformity.
Proposition 6.8 (Reconstruction of the compact Haar reference). At every endpoint in C⋆ , the exact
Wilson–Haar identity, the ownership theorem, and the connected-logarithm admission give three families
of scale-normalized factors:
   (i) reference factors qA  ref
                                 (u) containing the Gaussian precision, its compact Haar completion, and the
       complete allocated two-jet;
  (ii) regular residual activities rA (u);
 (iii) irregular core operators bX (u) after exact label recombination.
There are mref , areg , airr > 0 and fixed constants such that, with zero, one, or two exterior/source marks,
                                     X
                                 sup     emref diam A ∥qA
                                                        ref
                                                            (u)∥C 2sG ≤ Cref (u−1 + 1),                  (42)
                             e
                                 A∋e
                                       X
                                 sup         eareg |A| ∥rA (u)∥C 2sG ≤ Creg (uω0 + u3 ),                (43)
                                  e

                                  e
                                       X∋e

All constants are uniform in the microscopic depth.

Proof. Apply Lemma 6.7 at the directed crossing depth. Equation (HT2) places the product of all empty-
support Gaussian, coarea, and chart normalizations in the single scalar Zn ; it is therefore absent from
every rooted row. The nonempty complete two-jets collected into Qref n have the explicit depth-uniform
form
                                 n =u
                               Qref     −1
                                            Qtempl + Qtail
                                                       n,opt + Qn,fin ,
                                                                 tail
                                                                                                  (HT4)
                                                            
                                              opt

where the fixed optical template and both tail families have uniformly summable exponential rows with
their first two marks. The prefactor u−1 occurs once, not once per weak depth: (HT2) has already
removed all empty-support normalizations, and DAS2 uses the same scale-normalized optical template at
every step. Restoring product Haar measure converts the physical link logarithm only at the final scale.
The optical precision and its first two marked derivatives lie in the exponential matrix algebra. Summing
the finite-template incidences gives (42).
   The regular factors in (HT1) are exactly the species of Proposition 4.26. The invariant-graph cone and
output recertification therefore give (43). The recombined irregular row is bounded by Theorem 3.31,
giving (44). For the fixed group G, the Karcher, Haar, coarea, splice, and coordinate templates are
holomorphic or C ∞ through every order used here. Repeating the differentiated finite-dimensional score
and total-variation estimates for 0 ≤ h ≤ 2sG changes only the fixed constants. The permanent support
reporting is unchanged because these are internal pivot derivatives, not additional physical-source marks.
Reporting completeness in Lemma 6.7 proves that every atlas, partition, Jacobian, gauge-coordinate, and
inherited factor occurs in at least one row; canonical ownership proves it occurs in at most one. Thus the
three rows are exhaustive and disjoint, and all constants are independent of the number of weak steps.

Lemma 6.9 (Complete one-pivot score and oscillation). Fix one live pivot p, its path product, all nonpivot
details, and exterior data η. Recombine the exact regular and irregular labels before normalization and
integrate every owned fresh variable whose factor meets no other live pivot. The resulting partial weight
wp,η (U ) is strictly positive and C 2sG on G. Uniformly for u ∈ I⋆ ,

               sup ∥∇U log wp,η (U )∥ ≤ Csc (u−1 + 1),            oscU log wp,η (U ) ≤ CΩ (u−1 + 1).
               U ∈G




                                                          55
For every 2 ≤ h ≤ 2sG one also has

                                  sup ∥∇hU log wp,η (U )∥ ≤ CG,h (1 + u−h ),
                                  U ∈G

and the same hierarchy holds with one fixed exterior or source mark.
Proof. Use a unit-speed geodesic U (t) in the compact group and recombine the labelled partition before
differentiating. The original Wilson–Haar integrand is strictly positive, hence its owned partial integral is
positive. On every regular atlas branch, differentiation under that finite-dimensional integral gives the
exact normalized-score identity
                                                                                    
                      d                                d                 d
                         log wp,η (U (t)) = EU (t),η −    Qref + Rreg +    log Jcomp ,
                                                                     
                      dt                               dt               dt
where Jcomp denotes the complete Haar, coarea, coordinate-return, and owned normalization factor. The
support-summed first-derivative form of (42) bounds the reference score by C(u−1 + 1); (43) bounds the
regular score by a smaller fixed term.
   On a collar or irregular branch the same identity is applied to the recombined real integral. For every
owned core meeting the pivot, the plateau and differentiated total-variation theorems give the displayed
normalized ratio

Indeed the numerator is the same charged action exponential as the undifferentiated bad row times
                                                                                                  2
a fixed polynomial in u−1 , while the denominator is the trial-ball lower bound [c0 ud0 /2 e−CRg ]|X| ; the
action-dominance inequality absorbs that polynomial and the trial loss into bu (X). For a one-core reference
there is no Gaussian cross word; the ratio is therefore taken before the remaining product-reference
variables are integrated. Cross-core words remain in the explicit connected row. Summing (HT3) proves
the collar/irregular score row.
   Higher pivot derivatives are obtained by differentiating the normalized-score identity. They are finite
Bell polynomials in derivatives of the complete local action, Jacobian, and normalized cumulants. The
fixed-group atlas and the C ∞ splice templates supply these derivatives through order 2sG ; the plateau
denominator and differentiated total-variation bounds control each normalized cumulant without a
core-volume factor. Thus the hth derivative costs only a fixed polynomial in u−1 .
   Because u ranges in the fixed compact interval I⋆ , all remaining finite powers of u−1 are absorbed into
the displayed CG,h (1 + u−h ) hierarchy. A finite atlas covers the one-pivot compact fiber, so the score
constants are uniform in U , exterior data, volume, and preceding weak depth. Integrating the first score
along a geodesic of length at most diam G proves the oscillation bound. Marking one fixed factor uses
the corresponding marked rows.
   For a finite family of active straight macro-paths, keep every fine link on those active paths in its
original link coordinate. An active path carries one nontrivial retained Peter–Weyl coefficient, or one
bounded scalar probe whose first derivative at the origin is centered. Choose live pivots P = {p1 , . . . , pN }
among the original links of the active paths, with mutually disjoint buffered neighborhoods separated
by distance R and separated from all path endpoints. Condition on every nonpivot fine link, every link
outside the active paths, and all exterior/source data; denote this collection by η. Do not condition on a
retained path product carrying an active coefficient or probe.
   Assign every physical factor by its incidence in the original fine-link support: factors meeting no live
pivot enter H0 , complete owned factors meeting exactly pi enter the ith one-pivot weight, and connected
factors meeting at least two live pivots enter the multipivot remainder. The active retained carriers are
distinguished insertions and are never absorbed into a one-pivot density or counted as physical activities.
   Define

              Ω(u) := CΩ (ū)(u−1 + 1),                                                                    (45)
                         h                                                                     i
             ∆R (u) := C∆ (u−1 + 1)e−mref cp R + (uω0 + u3 )e−areg cp R + u−p e−c/u e−airr cp R ,          (46)

where cp > 0 is the original-support separation constant. The constant CΩ (ū) may depend on the fixed
crossing interval I⋆ , equivalently on its positive lower endpoint ū; downstream arguments consume only
the finiteness of Ω⋆ , never uniformity as ū ↓ 0.

                                                     18
3.6    Irregular ownership and C 1 flatness
The irregular map must be assembled from the exact labelled density before any absolute-value estimate
is taken. This section fixes the ownership rule that was previously left as four composition hypotheses.
   Let X = {X1 , . . . , Xm } be a compatible family of bad cores. Compatibility means that the enlarged
integration cores I + (Xi ) are pairwise disjoint. Every pre-logarithmic local factor carries its actual finite
support; every exponentially local inverse, coarea, or Schur factor is first expanded in the permanent
exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.
Definition 3.22 (Canonical owner). For a reported connected factor A with support S(A) define
                               
                               Xi ,
                                        S(A) ∩ I(Xi ) ̸= ∅ and S(A) ∩ I(Xj ) = ∅ (j ̸= i),
                ownX (A) = cross, S(A) meets at least two integration cores,
                                 reg,    S(A) meets no integration core.
                               
                               

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.
Definition 3.23 (Gaussian/determinant allocation separation). For the exact joint fresh-detail law, let
H0 denote the finite-range ambient precision before the Karcher constraint and let Qopt be the actual
constrained precision of Theorem 4.9. Fix a separation collar RG . Cores closer than RG after their
ordinary interaction collars are merged into one core. A remaining compatible family satisfies:
(DAS1) every determinant endpoint whose two-jet enters the reference has an actual support of diameter
         at most the fixed number Rdet ;
(DAS2) complementary projectors Pvac + Pret + Popt + Pirr = I allocate every endpoint two-jet exactly
         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;
(DAS4) H0 is finite range, while Qopt and its inverse have uniform exponentially decaying kernels;
(DAS5) for distinct compatible cores,
                    X                  ′

Lemma 3.24 (The exact forty-nine-path block satisfies DAS). The Wilson Hessian, local Haar and
coarea endpoint two-jets, the optical projector, and the compatible-core geometry satisfy Definition 3.23,
uniformly in finite volume and weak depth. The collar RG may be chosen once so that κG (RG ) < 1/8.
Proof. The Wilson plaquette Hessian and Feynman grounding term have fixed finite range. The forty-nine-
path constraint and each chart/coarea endpoint live on one fixed block template. The coefficient projectors
are the complementary projections of the complete two-jet extraction, and the stagewise conditionals are
chain-rule factors of one normalized joint law. The actual constrained precision is Qopt = T ∗ Hf T . The
analytic-strip proof of Corollary 4.10 applies to T , Hf , and Qopt itself, so both precision and covariance
have uniform exponential kernel rows. Restricting that row to two cores separated by RG gives (1).
Merging the finitely many cores that violate the separation condition preserves the action charge and
leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.

support Gaussian, coarea, and chart normalizations in the single scalar Zn ; it is therefore absent from
every rooted row. The nonempty complete two-jets collected into Qref n have the explicit depth-uniform
form
                                 n =u
                               Qref     −1
                                            Qtempl + Qtail
                                                       n,opt + Qn,fin ,
                                                                 tail
                                                                                                  (HT4)
                                                            
                                              opt

where the fixed optical template and both tail families have uniformly summable exponential rows with
their first two marks. The prefactor u−1 occurs once, not once per weak depth: (HT2) has already
removed all empty-support normalizations, and DAS2 uses the same scale-normalized optical template at
every step. Restoring product Haar measure converts the physical link logarithm only at the final scale.
The optical precision and its first two marked derivatives lie in the exponential matrix algebra. Summing
the finite-template incidences gives (42).
   The regular factors in (HT1) are exactly the species of Proposition 4.26. The invariant-graph cone and
output recertification therefore give (43). The recombined irregular row is bounded by Theorem 3.31,
giving (44). For the fixed group G, the Karcher, Haar, coarea, splice, and coordinate templates are
holomorphic or C ∞ through every order used here. Repeating the differentiated finite-dimensional score
and total-variation estimates for 0 ≤ h ≤ 2sG changes only the fixed constants. The permanent support
reporting is unchanged because these are internal pivot derivatives, not additional physical-source marks.
Reporting completeness in Lemma 6.7 proves that every atlas, partition, Jacobian, gauge-coordinate, and
inherited factor occurs in at least one row; canonical ownership proves it occurs in at most one. Thus the
three rows are exhaustive and disjoint, and all constants are independent of the number of weak steps.

Lemma 6.9 (Complete one-pivot score and oscillation). Fix one live pivot p, its path product, all nonpivot
details, and exterior data η. Recombine the exact regular and irregular labels before normalization and
integrate every owned fresh variable whose factor meets no other live pivot. The resulting partial weight

               sup ∥∇U log wp,η (U )∥ ≤ Csc (u−1 + 1),            oscU log wp,η (U ) ≤ CΩ (u−1 + 1).

For every 2 ≤ h ≤ 2sG one also has

                                  U ∈G

and the same hierarchy holds with one fixed exterior or source mark.

a fixed polynomial in u−1 , while the denominator is the trial-ball lower bound [c0 ud0 /2 e−CRg ]|X| ; the
action-dominance inequality absorbs that polynomial and the trial loss into bu (X). For a one-core reference
there is no Gaussian cross word; the ratio is therefore taken before the remaining product-reference
variables are integrated. Cross-core words remain in the explicit connected row. Summing (HT3) proves
the collar/irregular score row.
   Higher pivot derivatives are obtained by differentiating the normalized-score identity. They are finite
Bell polynomials in derivatives of the complete local action, Jacobian, and normalized cumulants. The
fixed-group atlas and the C ∞ splice templates supply these derivatives through order 2sG ; the plateau
denominator and differentiated total-variation bounds control each normalized cumulant without a
core-volume factor. Thus the hth derivative costs only a fixed polynomial in u−1 .
   Because u ranges in the fixed compact interval I⋆ , all remaining finite powers of u−1 are absorbed into
the displayed CG,h (1 + u−h ) hierarchy. A finite atlas covers the one-pivot compact fiber, so the score
constants are uniform in U , exterior data, volume, and preceding weak depth. Integrating the first score
along a geodesic of length at most diam G proves the oscillation bound. Marking one fixed factor uses
the corresponding marked rows.
   For a finite family of active straight macro-paths, keep every fine link on those active paths in its
original link coordinate. An active path carries one nontrivial retained Peter–Weyl coefficient, or one
bounded scalar probe whose first derivative at the origin is centered. Choose live pivots P = {p1 , . . . , pN }
among the original links of the active paths, with mutually disjoint buffered neighborhoods separated
by distance R and separated from all path endpoints. Condition on every nonpivot fine link, every link
outside the active paths, and all exterior/source data; denote this collection by η. Do not condition on a
retained path product carrying an active coefficient or probe.
   Assign every physical factor by its incidence in the original fine-link support: factors meeting no live
pivot enter H0 , complete owned factors meeting exactly pi enter the ith one-pivot weight, and connected
factors meeting at least two live pivots enter the multipivot remainder. The active retained carriers are
distinguished insertions and are never absorbed into a one-pivot density or counted as physical activities.

where cp > 0 is the original-support separation constant. The constant CΩ (ū) may depend on the fixed
crossing interval I⋆ , equivalently on its positive lower endpoint ū; downstream arguments consume only
the finiteness of Ω⋆ , never uniformity as ū ↓ 0.

from this collected output, so neither a factor nor its scalar normalization is copied at a later depth. This
gives (HT1) at j + 1. The normalized structural/source marks commute with the finite induction and are
bounded by the same permanent rows, proving depth uniformity.

Lemma 3.29 (History compatibility after exact output formation). At every RG step, form the complete
connected output first, assign its canonical reported hull, and only then compute its regulator allocation
and history certificate. Under this rule:
   (i) every input history has one output owner;
  (ii) no input allocation is copied to two incompatible output histories;
 (iii) concatenation of successive certificates is associative;
 (iv) summing over all certificates recovers the exact unlabelled connected output.
Proof. For one step, canonical collect is a partition of the finite set of labelled connected histories by the
least rooted hull in the fixed ordering. Thus there is one owner and the unlabelled sum is unchanged.
The output allocation is a function of the already collected output gradient and complex Hessian, not of
an input branch. Induct on the number of steps. At the next step the complete preceding certificate
is treated as one input label, so a new canonical hull is appended once. This proves associativity and
excludes copying. Forgetting the labels at every stage gives the exact iterated conditional logarithm by
Fubini and the connected exponential formula.

                                              {h : H(h) = Y }

partition HL . Hence collect is exact, and for every bounded linear localization operator AY ,
                                                       X

Proof. The canonical hull and history certificate are deterministic functions of the reported history.
Absolute convergence in the rooted activity norm permits bounded linear operations to pass through the
history sum.

Gauge-covariant local jet projector. For a rooted support Y , choose the canonical root cell r(Y ).
Parallel-transport every local tensor to r(Y ) along the canonical hull tree and write the resulting local
field in the finite normal-coordinate atlas.
    Let JD (Y ) be the finite-dimensional space of gauge-complete, signed-hypercubic covariant, Wick-local
jets of declared engineering degree at most D, including the extraction sector and the stable jet sector.
Lemma 4.20 (Discrete covariant localization). There is a projector

                                          LocD,Y : FY 7−→ JD (Y )

with the following properties.
1. Loc2D,Y = LocD,Y , and it is exact on JD (Y ).
2. It commutes with gauge transformations, signed-hypercubic symmetries, and aligned reflection.
3. For 0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
                                ∥ LocD,Y FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD ∥FY ∥[m] .
4. If LocD,Y FY = 0, then after canonical scale-L rescaling,

                                 ∥TL FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD L3−D ∥FY ∥[m] .

  The estimate includes both the nonlocal output and its newly generated local projection:

                ∥ LocD TL FY ∥[m] + ∥(1 − LocD )TL FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD L3−D ∥FY ∥[m] .

Proof. On each finite local template, take the gauge-complete Taylor expansion in normal coordinates
and scale-dependent Wick monomials, transport all coefficients to the canonical root, and average over
the finite stabilizer. The dual coefficient functionals use finitely many derivatives and finite-difference


                                                     32
moments. Transport along a tree of radius τ (Y ) costs at most a polynomial in 1 + τ (Y ), proving the
third row.
   If LocD,Y FY = 0, every declared Taylor coefficient through degree D vanishes. The integral form of
the covariant Taylor remainder begins at degree D + 1. Canonical rescaling gives one root-preimage factor
L4 and L−(D+1) from the smallest omitted engineering degree. Thus the gain is

                                              L4−(D+1) = L3−D .

The regular linearized block kernel is analytic on the fixed tube and bounded through the three active
marked rows, so integration and the local coefficient functionals preserve this remainder estimate. Applying
the projector after integration proves the displayed localization-commutator bound; in particular, a
remainder cannot feed the finite local block without paying the same L3−D factor.

  Equivalently,
                                              δD = D − 3 −→ ∞.
   The estimates are uniform in periodic volume and preceding weak depth. They remain valid with one
or two passive exterior roots and every fixed finite passive source order.


                                                        33
Proof. For the long sector,
                                              [m]                                               [m]
                                   X                                            X
                         sup                  cL (Y ) ≤ e−κR(L) sup                    eκτL (Y ) cL (Y )
                          b                                                b
                                    Y ∋b                                        Y ∋b
                               τL (Y )>R(L)

                                                        ≤ Cm LΓ e−κR(L) ≤ Cm L−3 .

   For the short sector, first apply exact collect and then the local projector. Linearity and absolute
convergence make this identical to localizing each history and collecting by its canonical hull. Every
stable remainder lies in the kernel of LocD , while every off-diagonal local/remainder block has the same
Taylor-remainder gain. Since
                                              τL (Y ) ≤ R(L),
the discrete covariant localization lemma gives

                                              CD,m (1 + R(L))pD L3−D

per collected activity. Summing the rooted envelope contributes LΓ , proving the short bound.
   The canonical root and history certificate are selected once, so neither hull reassignment nor collect
produces a volume factor. Uniformity follows from the rooted envelope and the finite local template
atlas.

Boundaries and aligned reflection. Use the corrected reflection schedule:
• block the two closed half-spaces separately;
• retain the reflection-plane link layer as fixed boundary data;
• use only path templates with longitudinal extent [0, 2] and transverse extent [−1, 1]3 relative to their
  anchor.
Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,
and short-row estimates hold on each class with one maximum constant independent of volume.
   The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.
   The labelled weak/collar/outer densities recombine pointwise before the Osterwalder–Schrader form is
formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

is block upper triangular with the displayed diagonal. Its entries come from finitely many derivatives of
finite local analytic maps, so they are finite uniform constants. The spectrum of a finite block triangular
matrix is the union of the spectra of its diagonal blocks.

4.5    Localization and exact collect
Fix one finite dyadic macrostep L = 2s . All fields are expressed in the scale-dependent Wick coordinates
of the stable-jet theorem. Engineering degree includes discrete derivatives and every declared active
structural mark.

Rooted histories and collect. Let HL be the absolutely summable family of connected regular
macrostep histories. A history h has a reported connected support P (h), a distinguished root, and a
canonical hull
                                             Y = H(h).
The hull contains the reported supports, shortest covariance connector paths, and the fixed finite atlas
collar.
Definition 4.18 (Exact collect). For a coarse connected support Y , define
                                                     X
                                        (CL F )Y =         Fh .
                                                       h∈HL
                                                      H(h)=Y

The history certificate is retained as a passive label until this sum is formed.
Lemma 4.19 (Collect is a rooted partition). The sets

                                              {h : H(h) = Y }

partition HL . Hence collect is exact, and for every bounded linear localization operator AY ,
                                                       X
                                       AY (CL F )Y =        AY Fh .
                                                      H(h)=Y

No history is omitted or counted twice.
Proof. The canonical hull and history certificate are deterministic functions of the reported history.
Absolute convergence in the rooted activity norm permits bounded linear operations to pass through the
history sum.

Gauge-covariant local jet projector. For a rooted support Y , choose the canonical root cell r(Y ).
Parallel-transport every local tensor to r(Y ) along the canonical hull tree and write the resulting local
field in the finite normal-coordinate atlas.
    Let JD (Y ) be the finite-dimensional space of gauge-complete, signed-hypercubic covariant, Wick-local
jets of declared engineering degree at most D, including the extraction sector and the stable jet sector.
Lemma 4.20 (Discrete covariant localization). There is a projector

                                          LocD,Y : FY 7−→ JD (Y )

with the following properties.
1. Loc2D,Y = LocD,Y , and it is exact on JD (Y ).
2. It commutes with gauge transformations, signed-hypercubic symmetries, and aligned reflection.
3. For 0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
                                ∥ LocD,Y FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD ∥FY ∥[m] .
4. If LocD,Y FY = 0, then after canonical scale-L rescaling,

                                 ∥TL FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD L3−D ∥FY ∥[m] .

  The estimate includes both the nonlocal output and its newly generated local projection:

                ∥ LocD TL FY ∥[m] + ∥(1 − LocD )TL FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD L3−D ∥FY ∥[m] .

Proof. On each finite local template, take the gauge-complete Taylor expansion in normal coordinates
and scale-dependent Wick monomials, transport all coefficients to the canonical root, and average over
the finite stabilizer. The dual coefficient functionals use finitely many derivatives and finite-difference


                                                     32
moments. Transport along a tree of radius τ (Y ) costs at most a polynomial in 1 + τ (Y ), proving the
third row.
   If LocD,Y FY = 0, every declared Taylor coefficient through degree D vanishes. The integral form of
the covariant Taylor remainder begins at degree D + 1. Canonical rescaling gives one root-preimage factor
L4 and L−(D+1) from the smallest omitted engineering degree. Thus the gain is

                                              L4−(D+1) = L3−D .

The regular linearized block kernel is analytic on the fixed tube and bounded through the three active
marked rows, so integration and the local coefficient functionals preserve this remainder estimate. Applying
the projector after integration proves the displayed localization-commutator bound; in particular, a
remainder cannot feed the finite local block without paying the same L3−D factor.

Exact local block and localization commutator. Let ΠD denote the full local jet projector on the
                                  [s]
macrostep activity space and let Ljet be the exact finite stable Wick-jet matrix of Theorem 4.17.

                                                                       [s]

            ΠD DΦL (0)(1 − ΠD ),          (1 − ΠD )DΦL (0)ΠD ,           (1 − ΠD )DΦL (0)(1 − ΠD ),

is assigned to NL and obeys the long/short estimates below.
   In particular, the macrostep envelope LΓ does not multiply the finite local diagonal.
Proof. On the finite local Wick-jet quotient, Gaussian integration is exact second quantization and
                                         [s]
canonical rescaling gives the matrix Ljet proved in Theorem 4.17. This is the local-to-local block.
   For every coordinate containing a localization remainder, the preceding lemma gives the L3−D gain on
short supports. Local jets that produce degree above D also lie in the same remainder because the exact
forty-nine-path symbol raises degree only by even positive amounts. Thus all off-diagonal local/remainder
blocks belong to NL , while the finite local diagonal is computed before the support-envelope estimate.

The six one-step maps.          The exact regular step is organized in the following order.
(E1) Normal chart return. Substitute the finite-volume analytic normal section into the local Wilson,
     Haar, inherited, and source factors.


                                                              34
(E2) Coarea logarithm. Append the field-dependent logarithm

                                                  − log det(I + DN P ),

     with the fixed linear determinant owned once outside the activity map.
(E3) Summed connected logarithm. Apply the simultaneous hard-core/Gaussian replica-BKAR
     identity and sum the exact 1/n! series.
(E4) Local projection. Extract the complete gauge-covariant Wick-local jet and retain the short Taylor
     remainder.
(E5) Canonical hull and collect. Report covariance paths and finite collars, assign each history to
     one canonical hull, and collect exactly once.
(E6) Output recertification. Derive the next regulator allocation from the actual post-localization
     output gradient and complex Hessian.

Lemma 4.24 (One common support exponent). Suppose the covariance connector has decay exponent
µ > 0, and the normal chart, inverse trees, coarea logarithm, and fixed local atlas have positive exponential
locality exponents
                                      mchart , minv , mcoa , matlas > 0.
Choose
                                0 < κ < min{µ, mchart , minv , mcoa , matlas }.
Then all six maps act in the same κ-weighted rooted support space. No per-step weakening of κ is required.

Proof. For a BKAR tree, report every covariance edge by a shortest path and every hard-core edge by its
fixed collar. The geometric reporting inequality gives
                                        X           X
                               τ (P ) ≤   τ (Si ) +    de + Cgeo (n − 1).
                                           i            e∈Tcov


Multiplication by eκτ (P ) leaves the residual connector decay e−(µ−κ)de . Every fixed chart, inverse, coarea,
and atlas branch is summable at exponent κ. Localization costs a polynomial in the rooted radius but
does not alter the exponent. Canonical collect is a partition of histories, so it does not duplicate a support
weight.

Concrete connected-logarithm admission. Fix 0 < ϵ < 1/6 and use the physical weak-field radius

                                                 ∥A∥phys ≤ u1/2−ϵ .

                                       ∥Hi ∥phys ≤ u1/2−ϵ ,       1 ≤ i ≤ r.                            (SN)

            F means the operator norm after the substitution Hi = u1/2−ϵ H       b i with ∥H

marked row below is a derivative in an unscaled physical unit direction.
Lemma 4.25 (One common analytic species tube). There is a radius ηcom > 0, independent of volume
and preceding weak depth, on which the Wilson word, exponential-coordinate Haar density, Karcher
normal return, coarea logarithm, and finite Schur endpoint are simultaneously holomorphic. On the closed
half-radius tube, their derivatives through structural order three and every fixed passive-source order have
finite fixed-template suprema.

Proof. Each species is a finite composition of multiplication, inversion, exponential, logarithm, the
finite-cell Karcher mean, and an inverse of one of the matrices

                                         I + DN P,          Hopt + B(A).


                                                        35
The path-atlas theorem supplies a positive holomorphy radius for the first five maps. The uniform
inequalities ∥DN P ∥ < 1 and Hopt ≥ 2I persist on a smaller positive tube, so the two inverses are
holomorphic there by a Neumann series. There are only finitely many bulk and aligned-reflection
templates. Taking the minimum of their radii gives ηcom > 0; Cauchy’s estimate on the closed half-radius
tube gives the uniform derivative constants.
   For each fixed local template define the finite constants CW , CHaar , Ccoa , Cchart , CSch as the suprema
on that closed common tube of the third derivatives of respectively the Wilson word, Haar logarithm,
coarea logarithm, normal return, and finite Schur endpoint after their scalar and complete two-jet parts
have been removed. Let Cinc be the largest rooted incidence number of those templates and let Cconn be
the row sum of the exponential covariance connector of Corollary 4.14. All these constants are finite and
uniform under the standing convention.
Proposition 4.26 (Atom-by-atom weak species ledger). Put
                         1                                              3
                  ωW =     − 3ϵ > 0,      ωcoa = ωchart = ωSch =          − 3ϵ,      ω0 = ωW .
                         2                                              2
The complete rooted mass of regular input species obeys

                                             ηW ≤ Cinc CW uωW ,
                                         ηHaar ≤ Cinc CHaar u3/2−3ϵ ,
                          ηcoa + ηchart + ηSch ≤ Cinc (Ccoa + Cchart + CSch )uω0 ,
                                          ηinh ≤ Cinc r,     ηalloc ≤ Cinc b.                            (6)

Consequently
                                    ηatom (u, r, b) ≤ Catom (uω0 + r + b),
with the same estimate after three structural marks and every fixed passive source order.
Proof. The Wilson action has prefactor u−1 . Its remainder after the complete two-jet extraction is
bounded by
                                  CW u−1 ∥A∥3phys ≤ CW u1/2−3ϵ .
The Haar logarithm has no u−1 prefactor and its post-two-jet remainder is O(u3/2−3ϵ ). The remaining
three rows have the same explicit power. Indeed, on the common tube,
                                              (≥3)
                          (≥3)
                         Rcoa  (A) [3] + Rchart (A) [3] ≤ (Ccoa + Cchart )∥A∥3phys ,
                                              (≥3)
                                             RSch (A) [3] ≤ CSch ∥A∥3phys .

                                                                  1/2          1/2
For the last line write the finite endpoint as log det(I + Copt B(A)Copt ); after the complete two-jet
is removed, the matrix-logarithm integral remainder is cubic, while ∥Copt ∥ ≤ 1/2 and B(A) = O(A)
uniformly. Substitution of ∥A∥phys ≤ u1/2−ϵ gives u3/2−3ϵ for all three rows. The convention (SN) gives
the same power after up to three structural marks, because every marked 

=== canonical hull hit 6 at 137582 ===
plates with longitudinal extent [0, 2] and transverse extent [−1, 1]3 relative to their
  anchor.
Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,
and short-row estimates hold on each class with one maximum constant independent of volume.
   The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.
   The labelled weak/collar/outer densities recombine pointwise before the Osterwalder–Schrader form is
formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

                                         BKAR
                                        Km    < ∞,               0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
uniformly in volume, replica number, forest parameters, and preceding weak depth.
Proof. Proposition 4.26 bounds the complete species mass by δBKAR . The definition of Calloc gives the
first inequality. The Gram replica estimate removes replica-number growth, and the exact Pruefer identity
pays the labelled-tree degree factorials. After the fixed support and Cauchy costs, every parent-normalized
child sum is bounded by Ctree ηatom ≤ 1/2. The connected tree series and its first three marked derivatives
are therefore bounded by the first three derivatives of (1 − χeff )−1 at χeff ≤ 1/2.

where Bm is the finite multivariable Bell polynomial supplied by the chain rule through order m.
Proof. Compose the six bounded maps. Through order three, the chain rule contains only finitely

Definition 5.7 (Actual supportwise marked stable fiber). For every rooted support X, let

be the complete local extraction and its complementary activity projector after zero, one, or two

  [2]       [2]
(EX )2 = EX ; on the finite local jet its complement is the already defined Πirr = I − Πext . It contains

The same identity holds after every finite passive-source mark consumed by the local background coordinate.
Proof. Step (E4) localizes each exact connected history, transfers the extracted jet to the finite coordinate
block, and retains the complementary activity. The exact Loc/collect dichotomy proves that localizing
before canonical hull collect is identical to collecting first and applying the projector on the final
                                                                                   [2]

 [2]                                          [2]      [2]

Lemma 5.9 (Marked two-quantum-leg reduction in the actual stable fiber). Let H = X HX be the
                                                                                           P
tangent from Theorem 5.8. Choose 0 < a0 < κ and η = (κ − a0 )/2. Then
                                         X
                                     sup    e(a0 +η)τ (X) ∥HX ∥a;3,2 < ∞.
                                        e
                                            X∋e

After the two background marks are taken, every finite-support background/quantum/ghost kernel of
HX has zero complete local jet below engineering degree five. In the reflection-even physical background
two-point sector, degree five is absent by signed-hypercubic reflection, so the first nonzero associated-graded
component has degree at least six. The two-quantum-leg kernel is a sum of at most C|X|2 rooted edge-pair
terms, with coefficients bounded by C(1 + diam X)q ∥HX ∥a;3,2 .

Proof. The exponential row is the differentiated invariant-graph bound with an unused support reserve.
Equation (19) removes, after the active marks, every local jet carried by Πext ; in particular it removes
the marked B 2 A2 row that invalidates an unmarked centering argument. At first order in the tangent
insertion, the one-loop identity L = I − V + 1 = 1 shows that every additional quantum vertex is
a tree attachment to the unique loop; after those tree attachments are collected, the insertion enters
the background two-point amplitude through one effective two-quantum-leg kernel. Two fluctuation
derivatives choose an ordered pair of edges in X, producing at most C|X|2 terms. Rooted parallel
transport and finite localization cost only a support polynomial. Reflection excludes an odd engineering
degree in the physical scalar two-point sector.
Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected
1PI subgraph with an internal line and the SI–6 subtraction degree; the effective two-quantum-leg HX
kernel is one indivisible vertex. After tree attachments are collected, the reduced graph is unicyclic and
every marked vertex lies on its unique cycle. Every complete BPHZL packet is exactly one of: (W) a
pure-Wilson packet; (H1) one marked insertion, with only pure-Wilson contractions or one mixed cycle
contraction; (H≥ 2) several marked insertions with the same alternatives; or (Hfar) a diam X > 1. A


                                                             44
mixed contraction is loop-free. A loop-bearing mixed quotient retains a marked kernel whose complete jet
through degree four vanishes and whose physical even component begins in degree six. Hence only (W)
can carry an unsuppressed P⋆ log a term; every row has polynomial support multiplicity, and (Hfar) is
O(aN ) for every fixed N after the rooted sum.

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

Definition 5.11 (Lowenstein infrared dimension at the coefficient interface). Give a vector, background-
vector, ghost, or antighost leg infrared dimension one, and give a lattice difference and the Lowenstein
variable 1 − s infrared dimension one. For a periodic vertex symbol V let νIR (V ) be its lower Taylor order
when all incident soft momenta and 1 − s are scaled together, and put

                               ρ(V ) := νIR (V ) + nA (V ) + nc (V ) + nc̄ (V ),

where background-vector legs are counted in nA . The SI–7M vertex condition used here is

                                                   ρ(V ) ≥ 4                                           (20)

for every elementary or reduced local vertex entering a Lowenstein forest. For a connected soft graph γ
whose massless vector and ghost lines have order −2, incidence and Lγ = Iγ − |Vγ | + 1 give
                                          X                        X

Lemma 5.12 (Complete infrared vertex and forest admission). Fix X with a diam X ≤ 1, a graph in
Lemma 5.10, q ∈ Q⋆ , and at most two background-amplitude marks. With Lowenstein parameter s, write
                                                X
                                  RGLZ
                                       (q, s) =     TFLZ IG (q, s),                            (22)
                                                   F ∈F(G)


where TFLZ is the ordered SI–6B/SI–7M forest operator. At s = 1 only this complete packet is evaluated.
  Every elementary vertex and every reduced vertex admitted by the forest satisfies (20). More explicitly,

                       vertex family                 νIR       number of dimension-one legs    ρ
             vector or ghost quadratic symbol         2                     2                  4
           three-vector or ghost–vector vertex       ≥1                     3                 ≥4
         four-vector, seagull, or tadpole vertex     ≥0                     4                 ≥4
             pure-Wilson reduced local vertex       4−n                     n                  4
           loop-bearing marked reduced vertex       ≥6−n                    n                 ≥ 6.

The two background marks create external legs and do not differentiate external momentum, so they do
not lower these orders. A mixed forest contraction is loop-free. A pure-Wilson contraction produces only
a standard dimension-four background-field vertex, while every quotient that retains the stable insertion
retains its degree-six marked vertex.
   Consequently every subtraction-generated exceptional configuration is covered by the SI–7M forest
theorem, not estimated as a separated Taylor piece. In particular, for the one-loop two-point classes the
complete list of soft degrees is

                                   ω vector bubble = 4 − 2 · 2 + 1 + 1 = 2,
                                   ω ghost bubble = 4 − 2 · 2 + 1 + 1 = 2,
                                  ω seagull/tadpole = 4 − 2 = 2.

These values also cover the exceptional zero-momentum configurations created inside the Lowenstein
operators. A loop-bearing marked quotient has at least two additional infrared degrees. Thus every complete
packet has strictly positive lower infrared degree in every soft subspace.


                                                      45
   The pure-Wilson ultraviolet degrees are the SI–6 degrees. By the one-cycle ledger, a mixed contraction
consumes the loop; otherwise the quotient retains a degree-six marked kernel and has ultraviolet excess at
least two. Hence all overall and proper-quotient ultraviolet degrees required by SI–7 are admissible. After
the finite SI–7M sector decomposition, uniformly in the periodic side M and through the two marks, the
grouped packet obeys
                                                       
                                       X
            RGLZ
                 (q, 1; k) ≤ C PX 1 +     dT4 (k, ξ)−2  ,     PX ≤ C(1 + diam X)p ∥HX ∥a;3,2 ,       (23)
                                      ξ∈SG

with the evident omission of PX for a pure-Wilson packet and with a finite soft set SG . A radius-r shell
contains O(M 4 r4 ) lattice momenta, so (23) contributes O(r2 ) after M −4 normalization. The dyadic shell
sum is uniform in M ; deleting a grounded zero mode only decreases it. SI–7 and SI–7M therefore give the
cutoff and s ↑ 1 limits of the complete packet, and the unused rooted support exponent absorbs PX before
the thermodynamic sum.
Proof. The quadratic, cubic, quartic, gauge-fixing, and ghost rows are the finite Taylor rows of the local

5       Invariant graph and universal coefficient
The marginal coordinate is u = g 2 . Inputs SI–6, SI–6B, SI–7, and SI–7M are used only to compute
the local perturbative Taylor coefficient of the complete fine Wilson vector-minus-ghost quotient. The
coefficient of the exact Karcher map is identified internally by evaluating one local standard background
coordinate on the two equal measures related by the exact Karcher pushforward, followed by unit-tangent
conjugacy on the centered invariant family. No unrestricted Haar-translated background functional, global
background-gauge section, or application of Reisz power counting to the optical conditional covariance is
consumed.
   In the canonical squared-coupling normalization,

The factor log 2 is the physical scale-two interval. The complete coordinate-intertwining theorem is
printed after the centered invariant graph, where exact measure equality and parabolic conjugacy identify
the one-step coefficient.

3.6    Irregular ownership and C 1 flatness
The irregular map must be assembled from the exact labelled density before any absolute-value estimate
is taken. This section fixes the ownership rule that was previously left as four composition hypotheses.
   Let X = {X1 , . . . , Xm } be a compatible family of bad cores. Compatibility means that the enlarged
integration cores I + (Xi ) are pairwise disjoint. Every pre-logarithmic local factor carries its actual finite
support; every exponentially local inverse, coarea, or Schur factor is first expanded in the permanent
exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.
Definition 3.22 (Canonical owner). For a reported connected factor A with support S(A) define
                               
                               Xi ,
                                        S(A) ∩ I(Xi ) ̸= ∅ and S(A) ∩ I(Xj ) = ∅ (j ̸= i),

                                 reg,    S(A) meets no integration core.
                               
                               

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.
Definition 3.23 (Gaussian/determinant allocation separation). For the exact joint fresh-detail law, let
H0 denote the finite-range ambient precision before the Karcher constraint and let Qopt be the actual
constrained precision of Theorem 4.9. Fix a separation collar RG . Cores closer than RG after their
ordinary interaction collars are merged into one core. A remaining compatible family satisfies:
(DAS1) every determinant endpoint whose two-jet enters the reference has an actual support of diameter
         at most the fixed number Rdet ;
(DAS2) complementary projectors Pvac + Pret + Popt + Pirr = I allocate every endpoint two-jet exactly
         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;
(DAS4) H0 is finite range, while Qopt and its inverse have uniform exponentially decaying kernels;
(DAS5) for distinct compatible cores,

Proof. The Wilson plaquette Hessian and Feynman grounding term have fixed finite range. The forty-nine-

chain-rule factors of one normalized joint law. The actual constrained precision is Qopt = T ∗ Hf T . The

have uniform exponential kernel rows. Restricting that row to two cores separated by RG gives (1).
Merging the finitely many cores that violate the separation condition preserves the action charge and
leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.

by a normalized one-core reference changes only the single-core vertices and normalization scores. Their
assumed upper/lower and score bounds are paid per vertex and do not alter the cross-link mass. The
connected logarithm removes every empty-support scalar once. This proves the stated uniform row in
exactly the marked norm consumed downstream.

3.6    Irregular ownership and C 1 flatness
The irregular map must be assembled from the exact labelled density before any absolute-value estimate
is taken. This section fixes the ownership rule that was previously left as four composition hypotheses.
   Let X = {X1 , . . . , Xm } be a compatible family of bad cores. Compatibility means that the enlarged
integration cores I + (Xi ) are pairwise disjoint. Every pre-logarithmic local factor carries its actual finite
support; every exponentially local inverse, coarea, or Schur factor is first expanded in the permanent
exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.
Definition 3.22 (Canonical owner). For a reported connected factor A with support S(A) define
                               
                               Xi ,
                                        S(A) ∩ I(Xi ) ̸= ∅ and S(A) ∩ I(Xj ) = ∅ (j ̸= i),
                ownX (A) = cross, S(A) meets at least two integration cores,
                                 reg,    S(A) meets no integration core.
                               
                               

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.
Definition 3.23 (Gaussian/determinant allocation separation). For the exact joint fresh-detail law, let
H0 denote the finite-range ambient precision before the Karcher constraint and let Qopt be the actual
constrained precision of Theorem 4.9. Fix a separation collar RG . Cores closer than RG after their
ordinary interaction collars are merged into one core. A remaining compatible family satisfies:
(DAS1) every determinant endpoint whose two-jet enters the reference has an actual support of diameter
         at most the fixed number Rdet ;
(DAS2) complementary projectors Pvac + Pret + Popt + Pirr = I allocate every endpoint two-jet exactly
         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;
(DAS4) H0 is finite range, while Qopt and its inverse have uniform exponentially decaying kernels;
(DAS5) for distinct compatible cores,
                    X                  ′
               sup       eaG d(I(X),I(X )) ∥Qopt,I(X),I(X ′ ) ∥[3] ≤ κG (RG ), κG (RG ) ≤ CG e−mG RG . (1)

                    X ′ ̸=X

Lemma 3.24 (The exact forty-nine-path block satisfies DAS). The Wilson Hessian, local Haar and
coarea endpoint two-jets, the optical projector, and the compatible-core geometry satisfy Definition 3.23,
uniformly in finite volume and weak depth. The collar RG may be chosen once so that κG (RG ) < 1/8.
Proof. The Wilson plaquette Hessian and Feynman grounding term have fixed finite range. The forty-nine-
path constraint and each chart/coarea endpoint live on one fixed block template. The coefficient projectors
are the complementary projections of the complete two-jet extraction, and the stagewise conditionals are
chain-rule factors of one normalized joint law. The actual constrained precision is Qopt = T ∗ Hf T . The
analytic-strip proof of Corollary 4.10 applies to T , Hf , and Qopt itself, so both precision and covariance
have uniform exponential kernel rows. Restricting that row to two cores separated by RG gives (1).
Merging the finitely many cores that violate the separation condition preserves the action charge and
leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.

3.6    Irregular ownership and C 1 flatness
The irregular map must be assembled from the exact labelled density before any absolute-value estimate
is taken. This section fixes the ownership rule that was previously left as four composition hypotheses.
   Let X = {X1 , . . . , Xm } be a compatible family of bad cores. Compatibility means that the enlarged
integration cores I + (Xi ) are pairwise disjoint. Every pre-logarithmic local factor carries its actual finite
support; every exponentially local inverse, coarea, or Schur factor is first expanded in the permanent
exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.
Definition 3.22 (Canonical owner). For a reported connected factor A with support S(A) define
                               
                               Xi ,
                                        S(A) ∩ I(Xi ) ̸= ∅ and S(A) ∩ I(Xj ) = ∅ (j ̸= i),
                ownX (A) = cross, S(A) meets at least two integration cores,
                                 reg,    S(A) meets no integration core.
                               
                               

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.
Definition 3.23 (Gaussian/determinant allocation separation). For the exact joint fresh-detail law, let
H0 denote the finite-range ambient precision before the Karcher constraint and let Qopt be the actual
constrained precision of Theorem 4.9. Fix a separation collar RG . Cores closer than RG after their
ordinary interaction collars are merged into one core. A remaining compatible family satisfies:
(DAS1) every determinant endpoint whose two-jet enters the reference has an actual support of diameter
         at most the fixed number Rdet ;
(DAS2) complementary projectors Pvac + Pret + Popt + Pirr = I allocate every endpoint two-jet exactly
         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;
(DAS4) H0 is finite range, while Qopt and its inverse have uniform exponentially decaying kernels;
(DAS5) for distinct compatible cores,

Lemma 3.24 (The exact forty-nine-path block satisfies DAS). The Wilson Hessian, local Haar and
coarea endpoint two-jets, the optical projector, and the compatible-core geometry satisfy Definition 3.23,
uniformly in finite volume and weak depth. The collar RG may be chosen once so that κG (RG ) < 1/8.
Proof. The Wilson plaquette Hessian and Feynman grounding term have fixed finite range. The forty-nine-
path constraint and each chart/coarea endpoint live on one fixed block template. The coefficient projectors
are the complementary projections of the complete two-jet extraction, and the stagewise conditionals are
chain-rule factors of one normalized joint law. The actual constrained precision is Qopt = T ∗ Hf T . The
analytic-strip proof of Corollary 4.10 applies to T , Hf , and Qopt itself, so both precision and covariance
have uniform exponential kernel rows. Restricting that row to two cores separated by RG gives (1).
Merging the finitely many cores that violate the separation condition preserves the action charge and
leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.

         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;

Lemma 3.24 (The exact forty-nine-path block satisfies DAS). The Wilson Hessian, local Haar and
coarea endpoint two-jets, the optical projector, and the compatible-core geometry satisfy Definition 3.23,
uniformly in finite volume and weak depth. The collar RG may be chosen once so that κG (RG ) < 1/8.
Proof. The Wilson plaquette Hessian and Feynman grounding term have fixed finite range. The forty-nine-
path constraint and each chart/coarea endpoint live on one fixed block template. The coefficient projectors
are the complementary projections of the complete two-jet extraction, and the stagewise conditionals are
chain-rule factors of one normalized joint law. The actual constrained precision is Qopt = T ∗ Hf T . The
analytic-strip proof of Corollary 4.10 applies to T , Hf , and Qopt itself, so both precision and covariance
have uniform exponential kernel rows. Restricting that row to two cores separated by RG gives (1).
Merging the finitely many cores that violate the separation condition preserves the action charge and
leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.

Proof. In the exponential matrix algebra, ∥DN P ∥ < η < 1 and the matrix logarithm has its absolutely
